❯ /findings-workflow:stage0-science                                             

● I'll capture the scientific framing before we touch any data. This is an
  interview — one question at a time — and your answers shape what I ask next.

  Let me start broad:

  What is the scientific domain and system here? Tell me about the field, the
  organism or sample type, and the measurement technology. For instance — is
  this a mass-spec proteomics study, and if so, on what kind of samples (cell
  lines, tissue, plasma…) and from what organism? Enough context that I could
  correctly interpret a finding later.

✻ Worked for 4s · done 12:08 PM

❯ Use of plasma EVs to assess molecular markers for Alzheimer’s Disease         
                                                                                
    dementia (ADD). These results are from a quantitative proteomics (DIA)      
                                                                                
    experiment that used Mag-Net to enrich for EVs. A pilot study evaluated the 
                                                                                
    diagnostic potential of the Mag-Net method using plasma from 40 individuals 
                                                                                
    divided among four groups: Alzheimer’s disease dementia (ADD), Parkinson’s  
                                                                                
    disease dementia (PDD), Parkinson’s disease with cognitively normal status  
                                                                                
    (PDCN), and healthy cognitively normal controls (HCN). PDD was included to  
                                                                                
    control for dementia-associated lifestyle changes and symptomatic           
  treatments                                                                    
    that could confound ADD-specific molecular signatures, while PDCN           
  controlled                                                                    
    for Parkinson’s disease and movement-disorder treatments without dementia.  
                                                                                
    HCN provided a neurodegenerative disease-free reference group. The study    
  was                                                                           
    designed to identify protein markers associated specifically with           
  diagnostic                                                                    
    group rather than nonspecific effects of dementia or Parkinson’s disease.   
                                                                                
    Groups were approximately age-matched and balanced by sex.                  

● That's a clean, well-reasoned design — the PDD/PDCN arms as controls for
  dementia-generic and PD/movement-disorder-treatment effects is exactly what
  isolates an ADD-specific signature from confounds.

  Next, the experimental design details:

  - With 40 individuals across four groups, is it ~10 per group, or unbalanced?
    Do you know the exact per-group counts?
  - Batch structure — were samples processed (Mag-Net enrichment) and/or
    acquired on the mass spec in batches, or all in one run? Was acquisition
    order randomized with respect to group?
  - Any QC or pooled reference samples (e.g. a pooled-plasma QC injected
    periodically), and any technical replicates?
  - Roughly when and on what instrument was this acquired, if you know?

  These determine what confounds I'll need to test for in Stage 1.

✻ Crunched for 8s · done 12:08 PM

❯ The samples were banked human EDTA plasma, not CSF, brain tissue, or          
  neuron-specific immunocaptured EVs. The study selected 10 ADD subjects and    
  compared them with 10 PDD, 10 PDCN, and 10 healthy cognitively normal         
  subjects from Stanford longitudinal neurodegenerative-disease cohorts. I      
  don't think how the samples were physically prepared and acquired are         
  relevant for this analysis. I would wait for the metadata phase to examine    
  batch structure or QCs and references. There are no technical replicates.     

● Understood — 10 per group, balanced, banked EDTA plasma from Stanford
  longitudinal cohorts, no technical replicates, and we defer batch/QC/reference
  questions to the metadata stage. That's the right call.

  Now the scientific goals — the heart of what shapes the analysis:

  - Is the primary aim discovery (find proteins associated specifically with
    ADD) or hypothesis-testing of pre-specified candidate markers? Or both?
  - What's the primary contrast you care about — ADD vs. the other three groups
    pooled? ADD vs. HCN specifically? Or a more structured comparison that uses
    PDD and PDCN as the confound controls (e.g. proteins that move in ADD but
    not in PDD)?
  - Beyond individual marker proteins, are you interested in classification / 
    diagnostic performance (can the EV proteome separate ADD from the rest), or
    primarily in the biology (which proteins/pathways)?
  - Is there a downstream use in mind — a manuscript, a candidate panel for
    follow-up, method validation of Mag-Net itself?

✻ Brewed for 8s · done 12:13 PM

❯ I am primarily interested in comparing the alzheimer's disease samples        
  (labeled ADD) to all other experimental samples. I am interested in           
  developing a binary classifer to predict ADD vs/ all other samples. I am      
  interested in finding features that distinguish ADD from the other samples,   
  either from the classifier, from differential analysis, or some other means.  
  As a secondary analysis, I'd be interested in the same thing for the          
  parkinsons samples (PDD and PDCN combined) vs all others.                     

● That gives me a complete picture. Let me write it up.

  Primary and secondary aims are both discovery + classification on binary
  contrasts:
  - Primary: ADD (n=10) vs. all others (n=30) — build a binary classifier and
    identify distinguishing features (via the classifier, differential analysis,
    or otherwise).
  - Secondary: Parkinson's (PDD+PDCN, n=20) vs. all others (n=20) — same
    approach.

  I'll capture the framing to state/PROJECT.md now.

● I now have a well-characterized picture — including a major confound. Let me
  present my interpretation for your confirmation before I write anything.
  Here's what the metadata encodes:

  48 samples = 40 experimental + 8 control references
  - Experimental (40): Condition ∈ {ADD, PDD, PDCN, HCN}, exactly 10 each —
    these are the 40 data-matrix columns.
  - Controls (8): Plasma Reference ×4 (CP01–04) and TPAD plasma pooled reference
    ×4 (KP01–04). These appear in metadata but are absent from the data matrix
    — the matrix has only the 40 experimental samples.

  Join key: SampleID (the TPAD#### codes are exactly the data-matrix column
  headers; join is complete, 40/40, no orphans).

  Column readings (my inference):

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │                Column                │             Reading              │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Chris ID, SampleID,                  │ sample identifiers / de-id       │
  │ Poston_Plasma_ID, Linkcode           │ linking codes                    │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Condition                            │ diagnostic group + control role  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Age                                  │ age in years (60–87.9; complete  │
  │                                      │ for all 40)                      │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Sex / isMale                         │ sex (redundant encoding — 0      │
  │                                      │ inconsistencies)                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Education                            │ years of education (1            │
  │                                      │ experimental value missing)      │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Freezer Box Position, KF Plate       │ sample-handling / storage /      │
  │ Position, Tube Type, Label, Volume   │ plating                          │
  │ (mL)                                 │                                  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Run Order                            │ MS acquisition order (17–85,     │
  │                                      │ interleaved among a larger run)  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Cohort                               │ source cohort/site (Kerchner,    │
  │                                      │ Poston, ADRC/PUC, PUC only)      │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Collection date                      │ plasma draw date                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ alpha-syn                            │ α-synuclein assay result         │
  │                                      │ (SSA+/SSA−/not completed)        │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ plasma ptau                          │ plasma p-tau measurement         │
  └──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

  What the confounding tests found (experimental subset):
  - ✅ Sex — perfectly balanced (Cramér's V ≈ 0.00). Not a confound.
  - ✅ Age — no material difference (Kruskal p=0.24). Education balanced
    (p=0.83).
  - ✅ Run Order — evenly spread across groups (p=0.98) — acquisition order is
    not confounded with group. (This addresses the batch/order question deferred
    from Stage 0.)
  - 🔴 Cohort — strongly confounded with group (V=0.52 overall; V=0.62 for ADD 
    vs others). ADD is 100% Kerchner cohort; PDCN is 100% Poston. For the
    primary contrast, "ADD" and "Kerchner-source" are almost perfectly aliased.
  - 🟠 Collection year skews with group (Kruskal p=0.026; ADD earlier ~2012.9 vs
    others ~2014) — travels with the cohort split, implying differences in
    storage/freezer time.

  The cohort confound is the single most consequential thing here: any
  ADD-vs-others protein difference could be a Kerchner-vs-Poston collection/site
  effect rather than disease biology. I'll record it as a caveat finding.

  Let me confirm the foundational interpretation with you:

  can masquerade as a biological signal.

    The matrix is near block-diagonal: ADD is entirely Kerchner (10/10) while
  every PD-spectrum sample sits in Poston/PUC (PDCN 10/10 Poston; PDD 7 Poston +
  2 ADRC/PUC + 1 PUC only), and only HCN spans both site clusters (7 Kerchner,
  3 Poston). Reading down any column shows a cohort maps to essentially one
  group or one diagnosis axis, so diagnostic group is almost perfectly aliased
  with collection site — any group contrast is inseparable from a site effect,
  exactly the confound the caveat records (bias-corrected Cramér's V
  group×cohort = 0.515, rising to 0.620 for ADD-vs-other and 0.828 for
  PD-vs-other).

● Now I have the complete data picture — including a subtle trap. Let me
  summarize before the decisions:

  Data matrix (data/protein-data.csv):
  - Orientation: features (rows) × samples (columns), 2343 proteins × 40 samples
    (all experimental — confirms the pooled references are absent,
    caveat 0003).
  - Feature IDs: UniProt sp|ACC|NAME_HUMAN (2340) + tr| TrEMBL (3). 83 features 
    are " / "-delimited protein groups (ambiguous — e.g. histone/tubulin
    families).
  - Scale: log2 (median 22.7, p1–p99 ≈ 16–32). Not linear.
  - Normalization: already median-normalized — per-sample medians span just 0.15
    log2 units (sd 0.033); sum CV 0.003. The data arrived normalized.
  - 1 YEAST protein (sp|P00924|ENO1_YEAST, enolase) at high, stable intensity
    (~32.8) — a classic spike-in standard, not human biology.
  - No CON__/REV__ decoys, no duplicate ids/samples.

  The trap — disguised missing values: the matrix looks 0% missing (no NaN, no
  zeros), but exactly 2 features carry a sentinel floor value of 0.373 —
  IMPA3_HUMAN (14/40 cells) and CD048_HUMAN (12/40 cells). The identical
  repeated 0.373 is a not-detected/left-censored placeholder, not a real
  measurement. These 2 features are majority-floored.

  There are four preprocessing decisions to confirm with you (these depend on
  the above and get applied in Stages 3–4):

● User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · The yeast protein ENO1_YEAST (enolase, stable ~32.8 log2 across all 
     samples) is almost certainly a spike-in standard, not human biology. 
     Exclude it from the biological analysis set? → Exclude from analysis
     · Two features (IMPA3, CD048) carry an identical sentinel value 0.373 in 
     14/40 and 12/40 samples — an encoded 'not-detected'/left-censored 
     placeholder, not a real log2 intensity. Both are majority- or 
     near-majority-floored. How should I handle them? → Drop both features
     · The data is already median-normalized (sample medians within 0.15 log2 
     units). What normalization should the analysis use? → None — treat as 
     pre-normalized
     · Should the analysis apply batch correction? Stage 1 established a single 
     plate/batch, and the only site/cohort axis (Kerchner/Poston) is confounded 
     with the diagnostic groups (caveat 0001). → The data are already batch 
     corrected on cohort using limma batch correction in R using the following 
     code: limma::removeBatchEffect(                                         
           self$nobatchProt,                                                    
         
           design = model.matrix(~Condition, data = self$nobatchMeta),          
         
           batch = self$nobatchMeta$Cohort                                      
         
       ) 

● That's a critical revelation — thank you. The data has already been 
  batch-corrected on Cohort via limma removeBatchEffect(design = 
  model.matrix(~Condition), batch = Cohort). That changes the data-state
  description substantially and interacts directly with the cohort confound
  (caveat 0001). Let me record the other three decisions (exclude yeast, drop
  the 2 floored features, no further normalization), and first verify and 
  characterize what that batch correction did to the data — because Cohort is
  aliased with Condition, this correction has important subtleties.

     … +6 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

● Verification confirms — and sharpens — the picture. The batch correction is
  real and behaves exactly as removeBatchEffect(design=~Condition, batch=Cohort)
  should: within the groups that span cohorts (HCN, PDD), the residual
  Kerchner-vs-Poston difference is now essentially zero (mean Δ ≈ 0.00). But
  this exposes the crux:

  ADD (100% Kerchner) and PDCN (100% Poston) are single-cohort — the cohort 
  effect is not estimable within those groups at all. So their correction is
  extrapolated from HCN and PDD, and the design-level confound (0001) still
  cannot be resolved by this correction. This warrants its own data-provenance
  caveat. Let me record the three simple decisions and write the data

    Stage-3 integrity-gate loader is complete, tested, and clean under
  ruff/mypy/pytest.

    ## Files produced

    1. **`scripts/scratch/project_loader.py`** — the project-specific loader
  module (kind: module). Provides:
       - `read_full_metadata`, `classify_conditions` — read the 48-row metadata
  and split it into experimental (40) / control (8) via an EXPLICIT allow-list
  (`EXPERIMENTAL_CONDITIONS = {ADD, PDD, PDCN, HCN}`, `CONTROL_CONDITIONS =
  {"Plasma Reference", "TPAD plasma pooled reference"}`); fails loud on any
  `Condition` value outside these six levels, and on a `SampleID` that spells a
  pandas NA token (e.g. `"None"`) — a defensive guard added after Hypothesis
  found it as a latent risk.
       - `read_data_sample_columns`, `reconcile_sample_partition` — read the
  data file's raw header (via `csv.reader`, not `pandas.read_csv`, since pandas
  silently renames an empty header to `"Unnamed: 0"`) and reconcile it BOTH ways
  against the classified metadata: an experimental `SampleID` with no data
  column is a "true orphan" error; a data column that isn't an experimental
  `SampleID` (including a leaked control) is an error; an optional
  `expected_n_control` gives an extra opt-in numeric sanity check (used with `8`
  for the real files).
       - `prepare_feature_id_header` — materializes a byte-faithful copy of the
  data file under `results/prepared/` with only the empty first header field
  spliced out and replaced by `protein`; every other byte (every id, every
  value) streams through unmodified. Fails loud if the first field isn't exactly
  empty.
       - `load_paired_dataset(data_file, metadata_file, ...)` — the faithful
  full load. Writes `results/prepared/protein-data.protein-id.csv` and
  `results/prepared/metadata.experimental.csv`, then calls
  `common.data_loading.load_wide_data` with `join_key="SampleID"`,
  `id_columns=("protein",)`, `scale="log2"` (recorded), `missing="error"`,
  `require_unique_features=True`, `expected_n_samples=40`,
  `expected_n_features=2343`, `order_by="Run Order"` (numeric-coerced). Returns
  a `(40, 2343)` `Dataset`.
       - `load_analysis_subset(dataset,
  drop_feature_ids=ANALYSIS_DROP_FEATURE_IDS)` — drops the yeast spike-in
  `sp|P00924|ENO1_YEAST` and the two >30%-floored sentinel (0.373) features
  `sp|Q9NX62|IMPA3_HUMAN` / `sp|Q5BLP8|CD048_HUMAN`; fails loud if any target id
  is missing; never drops a sample. Returns `(40, 2340)`.
       - `main()` — manual CLI sanity check (`argparse`;
  `--data`/`--metadata`/`--prepared-dir`), logs shapes/seed-free params.

    2. **`scripts/common/data_loading.py`** — an unmodified copy of the vetted
  `wide-data-loader` lib/ template (`Dataset`, `Scale`, `load_wide_data`, etc.),
  seeded per `conventions/script-registry.md` (only the `__script_meta__`
  header and docstring intro were adapted to the project format; the body is
  untouched, per the template's own instruction). `project_loader.py` imports
  from it via a `sys.path.insert` pattern copied from the project's existing
  `scripts/figures/plot_group_x_cohort_confound.py`.

    3. **`scripts/scratch/test_project_loader.py`** — 23 tests: hand-built
  fixtures (orientation/pairing/scale/id-fidelity, including a pipe-delimited
  and a date-like id to prove no coercion), `prepare_feature_id_header` unit
  tests (both quoting conventions + reject-nonempty-header), a Hypothesis
  property/invariant test (value count, exact value preservation, Run-Order
  ordering — filtered to exclude pandas NA-token strings and the reserved
  `protein` name, both discovered as edge cases during development), a
  planted-truth fixture (seeded `np.random.default_rng(20260915)`, known +2.5
  log2 delta recovered on one feature, ~0 on a null feature), six required
  fail-loud edge cases (true orphan, data column absent from metadata, duplicate
  feature id, NaN under `missing="error"`, unknown Condition level, missing
  analysis-drop target) plus extras (empty data file, single sample, Run-Order
  ties, NA-token SampleID, control-count mismatch), and a real-file
  reconciliation test (`data/protein-data.csv`/`data/metadata.csv`: exact shape
  2343×40, exact 10/10/10/10 group counts, exact `{CP01-04, KP01-04}` control
  set, a raw-value spot check, and the analysis-subset shape 2340).

    4. **`scripts/manifest.md`** — updated with rows for both new modules
  (status `scratch`).

    ## Reconciliation logic (one paragraph)

    The full metadata (48 rows) is read as text and split into
  experimental/control by an explicit six-level `Condition` allow-list
  (fail-loud on any 7th value or a blank/NA-token `SampleID`); independently,
  the data file's raw header is parsed with `csv.reader` (not pandas, which
  silently renames an empty header) to get its 40 sample columns.
  `reconcile_sample_partition` then checks set equality in both directions — an
  experimental `SampleID` missing from the data is a "true orphan" error, and a
  data column that doesn't match any experimental `SampleID` (including a
  control that leaked a column) is an error — so by construction the metadata
  rows absent from the data are exactly the classified control set, with an
  optional `expected_n_control` numeric check as an extra belt-and-suspenders
  assertion. Only after reconciliation passes does the loader touch the
  filesystem: it splices the empty first header field of the data file to
  `protein` byte-for-byte (no value ever reformatted) and writes the
  experimental-only metadata subset, both under `results/prepared/` (`data/`
  stays untouched), then hands both to the vetted `load_wide_data` template,
  which performs its own independent pairing/dtype/duplicate/missing-value
  checks and returns the certified `(40, 2343)` `log2` `Dataset`.

    ## Assumptions / data notes

    - No contradictions found versus the task spec — the real files matched
  exactly as described (empty first header field `""`, 2343×40 shape, 48
  metadata rows split 40/8, `Run Order` complete and numeric, no NaN in
  abundances).
    - One thing worth the code-reviewer's attention:
  `scripts/common/data_loading.py` is an unmodified copy of the vetted lib
  template; its own dedicated test suite lives upstream at
  `lib/tests/test_data_loading.py` and was not duplicated into this project (out
  of this task's explicit scope, which named only `test_project_loader.py`) —
  flagging in case promotion policy wants that test file copied in too before
  `common/data_loading.py` itself is promoted.
    - `results/prepared/` now contains the two intermediates from my manual
  verification run; they're regenerable via `project_loader.main()` and harmless

    ## Assumptions / things worth flagging
    1. **Nothing contradicted the single-state premise.**
  `state/DATA_DESCRIPTION.md` and findings 0001/0004 confirm: data arrives log2

    2. **No seed parameter.** This script has zero stochastic steps (load →
  de-log → contingency table), so no RNG is seeded; I logged this explicitly
  rather than adding a decorative `--seed` flag.
    3. **mypy cross-module resolution caveat (pre-existing, not introduced by
  me):** when `mypy` is invoked per-file (as the project's own gate does, and as
  I did above), `pyproject.toml`'s `ignore_missing_imports = true` means
  `common.*` imports from a file under `scripts/scratch/` don't statically
  resolve to the real `Dataset`/`Scale` types — they fall back to `Any`, so a
  wrong-type argument to e.g. `Dataset(scale=...)` would NOT be caught by mypy
  in this invocation style. I verified this is already true of the existing,
  previously-reviewed `scripts/scratch/project_loader.py` (same behavior, not
  something my new files introduce), and that running `mypy scripts/` as one
  invocation instead errors out on a real module-name collision between
  `common.colors` (imported by `plot_group_x_cohort_confound.py`) and
  `scripts.common.colors` (imported by `cohort_characterization.py`) — a
  pre-existing inconsistency in how different scratch scripts do their
  `sys.path` imports. I didn't touch either; flagging for the
  code-reviewer/orchestrator since it affects how much the "mypy --strict clean"
  bar is actually catching across the whole `scripts/` tree, independent of
  this task.

  edited — the confounding re-check only writes to `results/qc_states/` and

  stripes; and since group is confounded with cohort (finding 0001), any
  apparent group-tracking block equally tracks cohort and cannot be attributed
  to biology here.

  The report follows:
    # QC: Sex/Age metadata vs proteome — verification report

    **Question restated:** Independently verify that sample metadata annotations
  — SEX (primary) and AGE (secondary) — are consistent with the actual protein
  abundances in the 40×2340 log2 plasma matrix, and flag any sample that looks
  misannotated or swapped.

    **Bottom line:** No actionable misannotation or swap is detectable, but the
  proteome is too weakly sex/age-informative in this plasma dataset to
  *positively confirm* the annotations at the per-sample level. This is a
  **power limitation, not evidence the annotations are clean.** Everything here
  is capped **`exploratory`**. Hand to the **stats-reviewer** before any
  promotion.

    ## Method (and why it is the canonical choice)
    - **Sex — no Y-linked proteins present, so path #3 (fallback classifier).**
  Leakage-safe multivariate sex classifier: `StandardScaler` + ridge-end
  logistic regression (elastic-net `l1_ratio=0`, the best/most-stable config; a
  sparse `l1_ratio=0.5` elastic net reported beside it), **leave-one-out CV with
  all learning fit inside each fold** (scaler + coefficients — no leakage), a
  **1000-shuffle label-permutation null** (also the leakage detector), and a
  2000-sample bootstrap AUC CI. LOO with a plain label-shuffle matches the
  target: sex is a per-sample attribute, no repeated subjects, so no grouped
  folds needed.
    - **Age.** Leakage-safe LOO `RidgeCV` predicted-age vs annotated age;
  Pearson (Fisher-z CI) + Spearman, 1000-shuffle permutation null on r, and
  gross standardized-residual outlier flagging (|z|>3).

    ## (a) Sex-informative proteins found
    **None.** All requested Y-chromosome / sex-specific entry-name tokens were
  searched (case-insensitive substring over all 2340 features): DDX3Y,
  RS4Y1/RS4Y2 (RPS4Y1/2), UTY, KDM5D, USP9Y, NLGNY (NLGN4Y), IF1AY (EIF1AY),
  ZFY, TYB4Y/TMSB4Y, PRKY, TXLNGY, PCDH11Y, AMELY — **0 present.** Expected for
  plasma proteomics (these are intracellular/Y proteins). The decisive markers
  are simply absent, so the primary sex check could not use them and fell back
  to the multivariate classifier. Also confirmed at screen: **0 proteins**
  separate annotated M/F at BH-FDR q<0.1 (top univariate effects only Cohen
  d≈1.0–1.35, none surviving correction).

    ## (b) Per-sample sex concordance
    - **LOO ROC-AUC = 0.709, 95% bootstrap CI [0.525, 0.871]; permutation p =
  0.017** (one-sided label-shuffle null, null mean AUC 0.466); balanced accuracy
  = **0.602**; n=40 (21 M / 19 F). Test: LOO ridge-logistic, 1000-shuffle null,
  2000-boot CI.
    - Sparse elastic-net comparison (l1_ratio=0.5): AUC 0.29 (near-degenerate,
  selects ~2 features) — confirms there is **no small set of strong sex
  proteins** to select; the weak signal is diffuse.
    - **Interpretation:** the proteome carries a *weak-but-above-chance*
  distributed sex signal (perm p=0.017, CI lower bound 0.525 barely clears 0.5),
  but at 60% accuracy with errors in **both** directions (confusion: F→12F/7M,
  M→9F/12M; only 24/40 concordant) it **cannot adjudicate individual samples.**
    - **Mismatches:** the P(opposite-sex)>0.90 rule nominally flagged
  **TPAD1010** (PDD, annotated Female, P(male)=0.94) and **TPAD1009** (PDD,
  annotated Female, P(male)=0.92). **These are NOT credible misannotations** —
  the same classifier confidently mis-calls genuine samples in the opposite
  direction just as hard (e.g. TPAD1002 M→P(female)=0.86, TPAD1003 M→0.82), so
  at this accuracy a >0.90 "confident" opposite call is expected from model
  overconfidence alone and cannot be distinguished from ordinary error. I am
  **not** reporting any sample as swapped. (Note: the metadata doc already
  records Sex↔isMale internally consistent, 0 discrepancies; the proteome cannot
  override.)

    ## (c) Age consistency
    - **Pearson r(annotated, LOO-predicted) = −0.628, CI [−0.786, −0.394];
  permutation p = 0.634; Spearman ρ = comparable; R² = −0.12; RMSE = 7.2 y; 0
  gross outliers (max |z|=2.13).**
    - **The r=−0.628 is a pure LOO artifact, not signal.** The
  heavily-regularized ridge collapses to the global mean (predicted-age sd 0.6 y
  vs actual 6.9 y; range 71.7–75.2 vs actual 60–87.9), and the permutation null
  itself has **mean r = −0.47** (leaving out a high value shifts its
  mean-predictor down → systematic anti-correlation). Observed r sits squarely
  in that null (p=0.63) and R²<0 (worse than predicting the mean). **No usable
  age signal at n=40** — as anticipated (low power, no external training set).
    - **Gross outliers: none** (|z|>3). The largest residuals (TPAD1018 age
  87.9→pred 72.5; TPAD1009 age 60→pred 73.2) are the age extremes the collapsed
  model cannot reach, i.e. model failure, not misannotation. This method would
  only catch a gross swap, and none is present.

    ## (d) Overall verdict
    - **No sample can be confidently called misannotated or swapped on either
  sex or age.**
    - **Sex:** no Y-linked markers exist in this matrix; the multivariate signal
  is real but weak (AUC 0.71, p=0.017) and too poorly calibrated to flag
  individuals. The two nominal flags (**TPAD1010, TPAD1009**) are most
  parsimoniously ordinary classifier errors — do **not** treat as swaps. If the
  scientist wants to chase them, an orthogonal assay (genotype/karyotype or a
  targeted Y-peptide panel) is the only decisive route; the proteome here cannot
  arbitrate.
    - **Age:** cannot be verified or refuted from the proteome (no signal); no
  gross swap detected.
    - This is a "clean-but-underpowered" result: I could not confirm the
  annotations, only fail to find contradiction.

    ## Safeguards applied
    - **No leakage:** scaler, coefficients, and ridge alpha all fit inside each
  LOO training fold only; never on the full matrix.
    - **Null = leakage detector:** the sex classifier's performance collapsed
  under the label-shuffle null (obs 0.709 vs null mean 0.466), and the age model
  collapsed to its null — consistent with real (weak) sex signal and no
  leakage, and no spurious age signal.
    - **CV matched to target:** per-sample LOO (independent samples, no subject
  clustering).
    - **Reporting:** every claim carries effect size + CI + permutation p with
  the null named; both sex models reported (not just the better one); all tests
  run are in `summary.json`.

    ## Caveats / power
    - Sex classifier n=40 (21/19), balanced-accuracy 0.60 — underpowered for
  individual calls; AUC CI nearly touches 0.5.
    - Age: no external age model; regularized LOO on p≫n is near-degenerate —
  only gross swaps were ever in scope, honestly framed.
    - Batch correction (limma removeBatchEffect on Cohort, protecting Condition)
  did **not** include Sex/Age, so that signal is preserved — the weakness is
  biological (plasma), not a correction artifact.

    ## Artifacts (all absolute paths)
    - Script: `/home/mriffle/data-analysis/manuscript-findings-workflow/demo-ana
  lysis/scripts/scratch/qc_sex_age_annotation_check.py` (parameterized, typed,
  seeded RNG=20260915, ruff+mypy clean). Rerun: `./.venv/bin/python
  scripts/scratch/qc_sex_age_annotation_check.py`
    - Results: `/home/mriffle/data-analysis/manuscript-findings-workflow/demo-an
  alysis/results/qc_sex_age_check/` — `summary.json`, `sex_per_sample.csv`,
  `sex_mismatches.csv`, `sex_protein_search.csv`, `age_per_sample.csv`,
  `age_outliers.csv`
    - Registered in `/home/mriffle/data-analysis/manuscript-findings-workflow/de
  mo-analysis/scripts/manifest.md` (row `qc-sex-age-annotation-check`).

    Note: this project has no `conventions/`, `lib/`, or `result_io` module
  present, so I adapted a leakage-safe linear-classifier pattern locally and
  persisted results as JSON/CSV (compute is light — ~seconds for the ridge/age
  models; the saga elastic-net null was intentionally skipped for the
  *secondary* comparison model only). Created no findings and did not modify
  metadata, per the brief.

    Separately worth noting (from the existing QC manifest, not my analysis):
  the sample-correlation QC already flagged **TPAD1012** as a global
  low-correlation outlier (robust-Z −5.0) — a different QC axis, unrelated to
  sex/age, but the same sample worth keeping an eye on.

● The annotation check is back — and the honest answer to your misannotation
  question: no sample can be confidently called misannotated or swapped, but
  that's a power statement, not a clean bill of health. Here's what it found:

  Sex:
  - No Y-chromosome proteins are present in this plasma matrix at all (DDX3Y,
    RPS4Y1, UTY, KDM5D, etc. — all absent; expected for plasma). So the decisive
    sex markers simply aren't available.
  - A leakage-safe multivariate classifier (LOO CV, 1000-shuffle null) finds a
    real but weak diffuse sex signal: AUC 0.71 (95% CI [0.53, 0.87]),
    permutation p=0.017, but only ~60% balanced accuracy with errors in both
    directions.

    both PDD/Female) are not credible swaps — the same classifier mis-calls
    genuine males just as confidently, so at this accuracy these are ordinary

    misannotated.

    regularization artifact (the model collapses to the global mean; it sits
    squarely in the permutation null, p=0.63). No gross swaps detected.

  Verdict: clean-but-underpowered — the plasma proteome here is too weakly
  sex/age-informative to positively verify the annotations at the per-sample
  level. If you want to chase TPAD1010/TPAD1009, only an orthogonal assay
  (genotype/karyotype or a targeted Y-peptide panel) could decide; the proteome
  can't. (Separately, TPAD1012 remains flagged on the correlation axis —

  hypothesis-generating only): S100A8 | P05109 | S10A8_HUMAN (down in AD; Zhang
  2024, PMID 39159863); F13A1 | P00488 | F13A_HUMAN and F13B | P05160 |
  F13B_HUMAN (up in AD; Nielsen 2021, PMID 34531462). The two MS studies do not
  overlap.

    --- EV-QUALITY-CONTROL MARKERS (NOT AD markers — but these are what Mag-Net
  enrichment SHOULD detect; use as QC anchors on the plot) ---
    CD9 | P21926 | CD9_HUMAN; CD63 | P08962 | CD63_HUMAN; CD81 | P60033 |
  CD81_HUMAN; SDCBP | O00560 | SDCB1_HUMAN; PDCD6IP (ALIX) | Q8WUM4 |
  PDC6I_HUMAN; TSG101 | Q99816 | TS101_HUMAN; FLOT1 | O75955 | FLOT1_HUMAN.
  (MISEV2018; Théry 2018, PMID 30637094.) [ad-plasma-ev-proteomic-markers]

    =====================================================================
    CRITICAL CAVEATS FOR HONEST QC-PLOT ANNOTATION
    =====================================================================
    1. MS-DETECTABILITY: The neuronal ATN + glial/axonal markers (Abeta42/40,
  p-tau181/217/231, GFAP, NfL) are ULTRALOW-abundance in plasma — historically
  Simoa immunoassay or immunoprecipitation-MS territory, i.e. below the
  practical detection floor of untargeted plasma DIA-MS. The plasma proteome
  spans >10 orders of magnitude (Anderson & Anderson 2002, PMID 12488461);
  untargeted single-run profiling reaches only a few hundred–~1,000 mostly
  moderate/high-abundance proteins (Geyer 2016, PMID 27135364). No source
  reports untargeted plasma or plasma-EV DIA-MS detection of
  GFAP/NfL/Abeta/p-tau. Treat their detectability in THIS dataset as an OPEN
  EMPIRICAL QUESTION the QC plot itself answers — do not assume they are
  present, and do not annotate them as expected-detectable.
    2. PROTEIN-LEVEL ID ≠ BIOMARKER-FORM ID: A DIA hit to sp|P05067|A4_HUMAN or
  sp|P10636|TAU_HUMAN only shows some peptide of the parent was seen — NOT that
  the diagnostic Abeta42/40 C-terminal fragment or a Thr181/217/231
  phosphopeptide was observed. Verify at the peptide/phosphopeptide level before
  attaching any ATN-marker claim to a feature.
    3. TAU ISOFORM NUMBERING: UniProt canonical P10636 is the 758-aa PNS-tau
  isoform, NOT the 441-aa 2N4R isoform the biomarker literature numbers against.
  Literature Thr181/217/231 map to canonical residues Thr498/534/548 (verified
  against UniProt MOD_RES). If phospho-sites are reported against canonical
  numbering they will appear at 498/534/548.
    4. WHAT TO EXPECT: The EV-QC markers (tetraspanins, ALIX, TSG101, syntenin,
  FLOT1) are the proteins a Mag-Net EV-enriched DIA run should genuinely detect
  — good positive QC anchors. The disease markers above mostly sit far below
  them in abundance.

    Manifest updated with all three rows. No pre-existing corpus was displaced
  (corpus started empty).

● Precondition confirmed: integrity_gate.passed: true. Stage 4 — the exploration
  loop — is open.

  Given the primary aim (find features distinguishing ADD vs. all others and
  build a classifier), the natural opening spark is a differential-abundance 
  analysis — it yields an interpretable per-protein hit table and a volcano to
  react to, and feeds everything downstream. But two of our certified caveats
  shape how I'll read it, so let me be upfront:

  - 0004 (batch correction): the matrix is already limma-corrected on cohort,
    and we don't have the uncorrected matrix — so I can't run the ideal
    "batch-as-covariate, both-ways" check, and the pre-corrected values make DE
    p-values anticonservative. Every DE hit here is exploratory and leans on the
    label-shuffle null + blinded validation for real confidence.
  - 0001 (cohort confound): ADD is 100% Kerchner, so any ADD-vs-others hit is
    entangled with a Kerchner-vs-Poston site effect (and can't be de-confounded
    by adding cohort as a covariate, since ADD is single-cohort).

  I'll run the primary contrast as the opening move and bring you the results to
  react to. Let me dispatch it.

  [Subagent hand-back] The text below is the final report of a subagent this

  The report follows:
    ## PRIMARY differential abundance — ADD vs Other (plasma-EV proteomics) —
  DONE

    ### Question restated
    Which plasma-EV proteins differ between **ADD (n=10)** and **all other
  diagnostic groups pooled** — `Other = {PDD, PDCN, HCN}` (n=30) — the study's
  primary contrast, on the delivered 40-sample × 2340-protein log2 analysis
  matrix.

    ### Method (and why it's canonical)
    `differential_abundance(dataset, contrast="ADDvsOther", method="moderated",
  reference={"ADDvsOther":"Other"})` — the **limma-style empirical-Bayes
  moderated linear model** (the template default; variance shrinkage across
  features is more powerful and more honest at omics scale than per-feature
  t-tests, per `conventions/statistics.md`). Binary `ADDvsOther` factor built
  from `Condition`; `reference="Other"` makes the effect **log2FC = ADD −
  Other**. Per-feature output: effect + **95% CI** + p + **BH/FDR-corrected q**,
  over the 2340-feature family. eBayes prior fit: `s0²=0.262`, `prior_df=3.12`.

    ### Results (structured)
    - **n tested:** 2340 proteins (all finite; 0 untestable). n=40 (10 ADD / 30
  Other).
    - **Hit counts:** **363 at BH-q<0.05** (133 up in ADD, 230 down in ADD);
  **594 at BH-q<0.10**.
    - **Effect range:** log2FC −2.90 … +3.52. Correction = **Benjamini–Hochberg
  (FDR)** over the 2340-protein contrast family.

    **Top ~15 by BH-q** (log2FC ADD−Other, 95% CI, q, direction):
    | protein | log2FC | 95% CI | BH-q | dir |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | MP2K2 (MEK2) | −1.26 | [−1.64,−0.87] | 1.2e-4 | down in ADD |
    | USO1 | −0.78 | [−1.02,−0.53] | 1.5e-4 | down |
    | VPS18 | +1.70 | [+1.10,+2.30] | 9.0e-4 | up |
    | PTN6 (SHP1) | −0.89 | [−1.21,−0.56] | 1.2e-3 | down |
    | TYPH | −1.32 | [−1.85,−0.80] | 1.4e-3 | down |
    | EIF3L | +1.74 | [+1.05,+2.43] | 1.4e-3 | up |
    | IVD | +1.43 | [+0.85,+2.01] | 1.4e-3 | up |
    | UBP15 | −0.57 | [−0.79,−0.35] | 1.4e-3 | down |
    | UFC1 | −1.10 | [−1.53,−0.67] | 1.4e-3 | down |
    | KS6A3 (RSK2) | −2.90 | [−4.05,−1.75] | 1.4e-3 | down |
    | UBN2 | +0.79 | [+0.48,+1.11] | 1.4e-3 | up |
    | SURF6 | −0.76 | [−1.04,−0.47] | 1.4e-3 | down |
    | NDUV2 | −1.84 | [−2.60,−1.09] | 1.4e-3 | down |
    | SORCN | −0.90 | [−1.26,−0.54] | 1.4e-3 | down |
    | PAIP1 | −0.61 | [−0.86,−0.36] | 1.4e-3 | down |

    **Strongest effects (|log2FC|):** ADRM1 +3.52 [+2.00,+5.03] q=2.3e-3 (up);
  KS6A3/RSK2 −2.90 (down); TNNT1 −2.87 q=1.5e-2 (down); H1BP3 +2.71 (up);
  MOB3C-group +2.66; RS8 −2.61.

    ### Known plasma/EV protein? — an important honesty read
    **None of the prior-literature plasma/EV-AD markers is a hit.** I annotated
  every feature against a curated, outcome-independent marker set (from
  `research/ad-plasma-ev-proteomic-markers.md` etc.): the MISEV2018 EV-QC
  markers **CD9 (q=0.19), FLOT1 (0.29), TSG101 (0.38), ALIX (0.70);
  syntenin-1/SDCBP only marginal (q=0.052)**; the unbiased-MS AD candidates
  **F13A1 (0.56), F13B (0.76)**; and **GFAP (0.61), APP (0.95), L1CAM (0.82),
  NCAM1 (0.94)** — all non-significant with tiny effects. Instead the top hits
  are **intracellular / organellar proteins** (kinases MEK2/RSK2/SHP1,
  proteasome ADRM1, ribosomal RS8, mito NDUV2/IVD, vesicle-trafficking
  VPS18/USO1) — i.e. the signal is NOT in canonical secreted plasma proteins,
  which, combined with the caveats below, argues strongly against reading these
  as clean biology.

    ### Calibration read (the main honesty check, no null available)
    p-histogram: strong monotone **spike at 0** (bin[0] density **6.5×
  uniform**, 12.3× the tail) — real enrichment of small p is present. **BUT the
  null region (p>0.5) is DEPLETED to 0.53× uniform**, far below the ~0.7–0.95 a
  mostly-null proteome should show. **Verdict: "signal present but tail
  suppressed."** A tail this low means either ~53% of the proteome is truly
  differential (implausible for this contrast) OR the p-values are
  **anticonservative** from the documented variance deflation (finding 0004)
  and/or the ADD–Kerchner cohort confound inflating the small-p mass. It is NOT
  a U-shape. **Bottom line: the 363/594 significant-hit count is inflated —
  trust effect sizes + CIs over q-values.**

    ### Safeguards / caveats applied (all reported, none worked around)
    1. **NO cohort covariate** — ADD is 100% Kerchner (single-cohort), so Cohort
  is aliased with the contrast (rank-deficient); a Cohort term would make the
  design singular. Reported without adjustment; **the ADD effect is inseparable
  from a Kerchner-site effect** (findings 0001/0004). Every ADD hit carries this
  confound.
    2. **Matrix already limma removeBatchEffect-corrected on Cohort**;
  pre-correction matrix unavailable → the ideal
  batch-as-covariate-on-uncorrected / both-ways robustness check is
  **impossible**. DE on removeBatchEffect output **deflates within-feature
  variance → p/BH-q anticonservative** (consistent with the suppressed tail
  above).
    3. **Exploratory** — small, imbalanced (10 vs 30), confounded. Not
  confirmatory.
    4. No label-shuffle null (that's the classifier/regressor discipline; the
  eBayes moderation is the DE-family variance control). Controls already
  excluded upstream; missing-value/floored/yeast features dropped upstream (2340
  = 2343 − 3).

    ### Hard problems
    None with the model mechanics — the moderated fit is well-conditioned (2340
  features, prior_df=3.12, no singularities, 0 NaN q). The one substantive
  concern is interpretive, not computational: the **suppressed p-tail + confound
  + the fact that the hits are intracellular rather than secreted proteins**
  together mean the significance is not trustworthy at face value. This is
  surfaced in the calibration verdict and caveats, not hidden.

  Where this leaves us, and where I'd go next:

  confound/leakage detector we need. If ADD is predictable only because of
  residual site structure, a properly-nulled classifier is where that shows up
  (or where a real, above-null signal survives).

✻ Brewed for 1m 39s · done 2:49 PM

❯ Why did you choose the elasticnet classifier?                                 

● Good question — it's the workflow's recommended default first choice, but it's
  a choice with real reasons and real alternatives, so let me lay them out.

  Why elastic net as the first pass:

  1. It fits the p ≫ n regime honestly. 2340 proteins, 40 samples. A regularized
     linear model is the least overfit-prone thing that still does something
     useful here; the L1+L2 penalty does embedded feature selection and — unlike
     pure lasso — the L2 part keeps correlated proteins together rather than
     arbitrarily picking one of a correlated cluster and dropping the rest.
     Plasma proteins are heavily correlated, so that matters.
  2. It directly serves your stated goal. You want "features that distinguish
     ADD." Elastic net returns a sparse-ish set of proteins with signed 
     coefficients (direction), which I can cross-check against the DE effect
     directions — a protein flagged by both is more robust than one flagged by
     either alone.
  3. Interpretability + small-n discipline. At n=40 with a site confound, I want
     a model I can reason about. A linear boundary is transparent; a flexible
     model would overfit and be much harder to interpret under the confound.
  4. Same rigor machinery. It carries nested CV, a coefficient stability read
     (selection frequency across resamples), and the label-shuffle null — which,
     given the inflated DE significance, is the piece I most want.

  The important caveat on it: elastic net finds a minimal-optimal predictor, not
  an all-relevant set. A protein with low selection frequency is not
  necessarily unimportant — it may just be a redundant correlated neighbor the
  model dropped. That's exactly what Boruta (all-relevant) answers, which is why
  I'd run it alongside, not instead.

  The alternatives, and when I'd reach for them:
  - Linear SVM — if the elastic-net selection turns out unstable, or you'd
    rather not force sparsity when correlated features share the signal. Same
    seed, and I'd choose between them on a paired per-fold comparison (a
    difference within seed noise is a tie).
  - XGBoost (gradient-boosted trees) — if the class boundary is
    non-linear/interaction-driven. But it's more overfit-prone at n=40, its
    importances are unsigned (no direction), and it's harder to interpret
    causally — so I'd run it beside the linear model when a linear boundary
    underperforms, not first.
  - Boruta — the all-relevant complement, run alongside.

  So: elastic net first because it's the most honest, interpretable starting
  point for this data shape and your goal — not because it's the only right
  answer. If you'd prefer to open with the SVM, run Boruta in parallel from the
  start, or something else, I'm happy to. Want me to proceed with elastic net
  (I'll start the checkpoint), or adjust the plan?

    Volcano plot of the primary differential-abundance contrast, ADD (n=10) vs
  pooled Other {PDD, PDCN, HCN} (n=30), moderated limma eBayes model on the
  delivered log2 40×2340 plasma-EV proteome. x = log2 fold change (ADD − Other);
  y = −log10(BH-FDR q). Each point is one of 2340 proteins. Points above the
  solid q=0.05 line are significant and colored by direction (vermillion =
  higher in ADD, gray = higher in Other); points below are background gray.
  Dashed line q=0.10; dotted verticals |log2FC|=1. The 11 known plasma/EV AD
  reference proteins are overlaid in a distinct color+shape by class (orange
  circle = EV-QC anchor MISEV2018; blue square = AD-marker parent protein; green
  triangle = EV-cargo/candidate) — all fall at or below q=0.05 (syntenin/SDCBP
  is the closest, q=0.052). EXPLORATORY: ADD is 100% Kerchner cohort, so the
  effect is inseparable from a collection-site effect and no cohort covariate is
  possible (single-cohort; findings 0001/0004); the delivered matrix is already
  batch-corrected on cohort, so the q-values are anticonservative (null p-tail
  suppressed to ~0.53× uniform) and the 363 proteins at q<0.05 / 594 at q<0.10
  are an inflated count — read effect sizes and 95% CIs, not q.

    === READING (assumes the legend image sits directly beneath the figure) ===
    The volcano contrasts ADD against all other diagnoses: proteins higher in
  ADD sit to the right (vermillion), higher in Other to the left (gray), with
  statistical strength rising up the y-axis. The strongest, most-significant
  hits — labeled MEK2, USO1, VPS18, SHP1, RSK2, NDUFV2, EIF3L, IVD, ADRM1 — are
  intracellular proteins, not the canonical secreted AD biomarkers. Crucially,
  look at the bottom of the plot: every known plasma/EV AD reference marker
  (CD9, FLOT1, TSG101, ALIX/PDCD6IP, syntenin/SDCBP, GFAP, APP, L1CAM, NCAM1,
  F13A1, F13B) sits at or below the q=0.05 line — none is significant — so the
  apparent 363-hit signal is dominated by proteins with no established plasma-EV
  AD rationale and is, per the title caveats, confounded with cohort/site and
  built on anticonservative q-values; interpret via effect sizes and CIs, not
  the hit count.

    # PRIMARY classification — ADD vs Other, FIRST PASS (no null)

    **Question.** Can the plasma-EV proteome (40 samples × 2340 log2 proteins,
  median-normalized + Cohort-batch-corrected) predict **ADD (n=10) vs Other
  {PDD,PDCN,HCN} (n=30)**? First pass only: fit + nested-CV performance +
  coefficient stability for BOTH linear classifiers on a shared seed, paired per
  fold. No label-shuffle null yet.

    **Method (canonical choice).** Two leakage-safe *linear* classifiers, seeded
  verbatim from the vetted templates and run identically:
    - **Elastic-net logistic** (`classify`, from
  `lib/analysis/classification`@0.3) — sparse/signed, the default first model
  (answers "which few proteins carry the class?").
    - **Linear SVM** (`classify_svm`, from
  `lib/analysis/classification-svm`@0.3) — dense/signed max-margin, offered
  alongside for a module-level reading.
    Both: nested CV (tune inner, estimate outer), **in-fold `StandardScaler`**
  (leakage-safe), `class_weight="balanced"`,
  `binarize=LevelMap(positive=("ADD",), negative=("PDD","PDCN","HCN"))`,
  `generalization_target="individuals"`, **stratified 5-fold × 5 repeats at the
  row level, no groups** (subjects are singletons → a held-out row IS a held-out
  individual; matches the Stage-4 checkpoint), **same seed 20260915**,
  `run_null=False`. Same-seed shared outer folds verified (identical
  `test_indices` per fold) → the AUC comparison is genuinely paired.

    ## Results as structured measurements

    **Elastic net** — cache `cbd809ac6168`
  (`results/classification/cbd809ac6168/`)
    | metric | value | uncertainty | test / n |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | nested ROC-AUC (unseen individuals) | 0.940 | fold SD 0.090; per-fold
  range 0.75–1.00 | repeated stratified 5×5 CV, n=40 (10/30) |
    | balanced accuracy | 0.830 | — | thresh 0.5 |
    | PR-AUC (avg precision) | 0.870 | — | imbalance-appropriate |
    | pooled out-of-fold MCC | 0.633 | — | pooled hard labels, 200 held-out
  preds |
    | tuned hyperparams | C=1.0, l1_ratio=0.25 (ridge-leaning) | — | inner-CV
  argmax |
    | non-zero features | 257 | — | minimal-optimal set |

    **Linear SVM** — cache `cba84421b85e`
  (`results/classification_svm/cba84421b85e/`)
    | metric | value | uncertainty | test / n |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | nested ROC-AUC (unseen individuals) | 0.963 | fold SD 0.067; per-fold
  range 0.75–1.00 | repeated stratified 5×5 CV, n=40 |

    | PR-AUC | 0.920 | — | imbalance-appropriate |

    | tuned C / support vectors | C=0.001 (heavy reg.), 22 SV (12 neg/10 pos) |
  plateau_start 0.001 | — |

    I report fold SD + per-fold range as the honest uncertainty; a formal AUC CI
  (bootstrap) naturally comes with the null pass — at n=10-positive the
  per-fold AUC swings 0.75–1.00, so point estimates are soft.

    **Every test run (both models, all reported):** nested ROC-AUC, per-fold
  AUCs (25 each), balanced accuracy, PR-AUC, pooled MCC, coefficient/weight
  stability loop, paired per-fold AUC comparison + Wilcoxon signed-rank, DE
  overlap + sign-agreement cross-checks. Nothing dropped.

    ## Paired per-fold comparison (25 shared folds, identical splits)
    - **Mean EN − SVM AUC diff = −0.0233** (SVM nominally higher), median 0.000,
  sd 0.060.
    - Fold outcomes vs the ±0.03 seed-noise band: **EN wins 0, SVM wins 4, tie
  21** (21/25 folds have *identical* AUC).
    - Wilcoxon signed-rank **p = 0.068**.
    - **Verdict: TIE.** The mean difference sits inside the ±0.03 seed-noise
  band for a ~40-sample nested AUC — not decided on the higher mean. Choose by
  the reading needed: **sparse minimal set → elastic net; dense
  co-regulated-module weights → SVM.** Neither is the all-relevant answer (that
  is Boruta).

    ## Stability reads (both flagged "NOT yet tested against a null")
    - **Elastic net (selection frequency + sign consistency):** the top set is
  very stable — top ~25 features nearly all have **selection_frequency 1.0** and
  **sign_consistency 1.0**, tight coef IQRs. Top signed: PCKGM (−, toward
  Other), PTN6 (−), C42S2 (+, ADD), UBN2 (+), USO1 (−), NEK6/7 (−, 0.98), MP2K2
  (−), JMJD6 (−), PRS10 (−), GOSR2 (−), PSMD3 (−), VPS18 (+), IVD (+).
    - **Linear SVM (top-k=20 membership + sign consistency):** dense, so top-k
  frequencies are diffuse (0.86 → ~0.2) with **sign_consistency 1.0** throughout
  — weight is spread across the co-regulated cluster (ridge-like), exactly the
  expected divergence from L1. Same leading proteins surface (PCKGM 0.86, PAIP1
  0.76, UBN2 0.56, RUXGL 0.66, EF1D 0.56, NEK6/7).

    ## Cross-checks (interpretation)
    - **Overlap with the moderated DE hits**
  (`results/differential_abundance/add-vs-other-moderated/contrast_table.csv`,
  363 at BH-q<0.05): **EN top-25 → 23/25 are DE hits** (the 2 misses are
  borderline: PCKGM q=0.052, NEK6/7 q=0.076). **SVM top-25 → 14/25** are DE hits
  (its denser ranking pulls in more module-shared / borderline features, e.g.
  HIBCH q=0.087, CTND1 q=0.42).
    - **Direction agreement (EN/SVM coef sign toward ADD vs DE log2FC(ADD−Other)
  sign): 25/25 = 100%** for both models. The classifier and the univariate DE
  agree on direction everywhere they overlap.
    - **Intracellular/organellar, not secreted/EV — yes, qualitatively
  confirmed.** The top discriminative proteins are overwhelmingly
  intracellular/organellar: mitochondrial (PCKGM/PEPCK-M, NDUV2/complex-I,
  ECHA/HADHA, IVD, HIBCH; ACOX1 peroxisomal), proteasome (PSMD3, PRS10/PSMC6,
  PRS6B/PSMC4), Golgi/ESCRT/vesicle-trafficking (USO1, GOSR2, VPS18, CHMP6), and
  cytosolic/nuclear signalling enzymes (MP2K2/MEK2, PTN6/SHP-1, JMJD6, CUL3) —
  **not** canonical secreted-plasma or EV-surface markers. This mirrors the DE
  top-hits characterization. Caveat: this is an identity read of gene names; the
  rigorous confirmation is a GO-CC over-representation run, which has **not**
  been done. Also note most top proteins are **down in ADD** (negative coef,
  toward Other), a minority up (C42S2, UBN2, VPS18, IVD, ECHA, CUL3, GCYA1…).

  ResultSchemaWarning); figures can render from the cache without recompute.

    ## Safeguards applied
    - **No leakage:** all scaling is in-fold via `Pipeline` (fit on training
  fold only); nested CV (inner tune / outer estimate).
    - **CV matched to target:** stratified row-level folds = individual-level
  (singleton subjects); reported strictly as "unseen individuals."
    - **Imbalance handled:** stratified folds, `class_weight="balanced"`, and
  imbalance-appropriate metrics (PR-AUC, balanced accuracy, MCC) reported —
  never bare accuracy.
    - **Paired comparison** on shared folds (verified identical test indices),
  read against seed noise — not the two means.

    ## Power / confound caveats (stated, not worked around)
    - **EXPLORATORY, capped.** Performance is **not yet tested against a
  label-shuffle null** → finding capped `exploratory`; coefficients/weights are
  **not yet licensed** ("not tested against a null").
    - **Cohort confound (hard bound).** ADD = 100% Kerchner cohort (finding
  0001): among these 40, "ADD" and "Kerchner" are the **same partition**, so
  above-null AUC **cannot** be attributed to disease vs collection-site — the
  model may be reading Kerchner as much as ADD. **The label-shuffle null does
  NOT resolve this** (it permutes the very partition that is both ADD and
  Kerchner).
    - **Small n (10 positives):** wide CV CIs; per-fold AUC ranges 0.75–1.00.
    - **Pre-batch-corrected matrix** (finding 0004); pre-correction matrix
  unavailable.

    ## Model misbehavior on the p≫n pre-corrected matrix (reported, per
  instruction)
    - **Elastic net:** the saga solver emitted repeated `ConvergenceWarning`s
  (did not converge at `max_iter=5000`) — expected on a 2340-feature,
  ~32-training-sample standardized matrix. AUC and the stability picture are
  robust, but **exact coefficient magnitudes should be read as directional, not
  precise**. A follow-up could raise `max_iter`.
    - **Linear SVM:** fired `TuningNoiseWarning` — **13 of 25 outer folds tuned
  C below the all-data plateau start (0.001)**; the default grid reaches further
  down (1e-5) than tiny inner folds can resolve, so the **0.963 nested AUC is
  likely slightly deflated by tuning noise, not raised by data**. The fix is to
  narrow `c_grid` to bracket the ~1e-3 knee and re-run (naturally folded into
  the null pass).

    ## Immediate next step (recommended)
    Run the **label-shuffle null** for both (`run_null=True`) — required before
  any strong claim and the built-in **leakage detector** (if AUC doesn't
  collapse under a permuted target there is leakage or no signal). State plainly
  it will **not** break the ADD/Kerchner confound. Pair it with a narrowed SVM
  `c_grid` (and optionally higher EN `max_iter`). Then hand to the
  **stats-reviewer** before promoting any finding.

● The classifier results are in — and they're a textbook case of why the null
  and the confound matter. Here's the honest picture:

  Performance (first pass, no null yet):

  ┌───────────┬───────────────────────────────┬────────────┬────────┬──────┐
  │   Model   │        Nested ROC-AUC         │ Balanced   │ PR-AUC │ MCC  │
  │           │                               │    acc     │        │      │
  ├───────────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────┼──────┤
  │ Elastic   │ 0.940 (fold SD 0.09, range    │ 0.83       │ 0.87   │ 0.63 │
  │ net       │ 0.75–1.00)                    │            │        │      │
  ├───────────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────┼──────┤
  │ Linear    │ 0.963 (range 0.75–1.00)       │ 0.74       │ 0.92   │ 0.52 │
  │ SVM       │                               │            │        │      │
  └───────────┴───────────────────────────────┴────────────┴────────┴──────┘

  - EN vs SVM: a tie — mean AUC diff 0.023 (within seed noise), 21/25 folds
    identical, Wilcoxon p=0.068. Choose by the reading you want (EN = sparse
    signed set; SVM = dense module weights).
  - The selected features are stable and coherent: EN's top ~25 have selection
    frequency 1.0 and sign consistency 1.0; 23/25 are also DE hits, direction
    agreement with DE is 100%, they're predominantly intracellular/organellar
    (mitochondrial PCKGM/NDUFV2/IVD, proteasome, vesicle-trafficking USO1/VPS18,
    signalling MEK2/SHP-1), and most are down in ADD.

  The two caveats that dominate interpretation:
  1. Not tested against a null yet → exploratory; coefficients not yet licensed.
  2. The hard confound bound: among these 40 samples, "ADD" and "Kerchner" are
     the same partition. So AUC 0.94–0.96 is exactly what you'd expect whether
     the signal is ADD biology or a Kerchner-vs-Poston site effect — and the 
     label-shuffle null cannot separate them (it permutes the very partition
     that is both).

  So an AUC that looks like a great biomarker is, honestly, not yet
  distinguishable from "the proteome can tell which collection site a sample
  came from."

  coefficient-stability figures to record this as a candidate finding. Let me do

● Agent "ADD vs other elastic-net + SVM classifiers" finished · 21m 3s

● Registration and the ROC + coefficient-stability figures are underway; I'll
  record this as a candidate finding once they're reviewed.

  Now the important decision about where to go next. The standard follow-up is
  the label-shuffle null — and we should run it (it's required before any strong
  claim and is the leakage detector). But given the confound, the null alone
  leaves the central question unanswered: is this ADD biology or the Kerchner 
  site? The null can't tell us, because it permutes the very partition that is
  both.

  The test that can address it is the confound probe: train the same classifier
  to predict Kerchner vs Poston among the 30 non-ADD samples (HCN and PDD span
  both sites). If the proteome predicts collection site at a similar AUC, that's
  strong direct evidence the ADD "signal" is substantially site — the most
  honest thing we can do here.

  My recommendation is to run both: the null (both models, with the
  statistician's suggested tuning fixes) and the confound probe.

● This is the decisive result. Here it is, honestly:

  The classifiers beat the null — this is real, not leakage:

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────┬───────────────────┬────────┐
  │    Model    │ Observed AUC │  Null mean (95%)  │ Perm p │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼───────────────────┼────────┤

  └─────────────┴──────────────┴───────────────────┴────────┘

  So there is a real, reproducible above-null proteomic signal separating ADD
  from the others — leakage and pure overfitting are ruled out. (The EN
  convergence fix worked; the SVM still has residual tuning noise, flagged for
  the stats-reviewer, but AUC is unchanged.)

  But the site probe cannot clear the confound:
  - Predicting Kerchner-vs-Poston among the 30 non-ADD samples: AUC 0.34 / 
    observed 0.475, null 0.50, p=0.98 — collapses to chance. The batch
    correction removed detectable within-non-ADD site structure.
  - This does NOT exonerate the ADD result. ADD is single-cohort, so its
    correction was extrapolated, not estimated (0004). "No residual site signal
    among the groups that span sites" cannot be turned into "the ADD signal is
    clean."

  Honest bottom line: there's a genuine, reproducible, null-beating signal that
  distinguishes ADD — stable, agreeing with the DE, sitting in intracellular
  proteins mostly lower in ADD. But it remains fundamentally confounded with 
  collection site (ADD = 100% Kerchner), and this dataset cannot resolve whether
  it's disease or site — the one test that could (the site probe) is blocked by
  the extrapolated correction. That's the ceiling.

  Let me register the new results and render the null-overlay ROC, the

    ## Caption (→ figures[].caption)
    Label-shuffle permutation null vs observed ROC-AUC for the primary
  ADD-vs-pooled-Other classifiers (elastic-net logistic, left; linear SVM,
  right), rendered from the cached null-pass results. In each panel the gray
  histogram is the AUC of 1000 label permutations shuffled at the unit
  (individual) level and evaluated at fixed hyperparameters (x = ROC AUC under
  permuted labels; y = permutation count); the dashed gray line marks chance
  (AUC = 0.5); the vermillion line marks the observed fixed-hyperparameter AUC
  (EN 0.956, SVM 0.972) with the empirical permutation p (both p = 0.001). Boxes
  also report the honest nested-CV AUC (EN 0.940 ± 0.09, SVM 0.963 ± 0.07) and
  the null centre/spread (EN mean 0.502, 95% [0.244, 0.778]; SVM mean 0.500, 95%
  [0.228, 0.756]). n = 40 (10 ADD, 30 Other). Observed AUC beats the null (p =
  0.001) → the discrimination is NOT an artifact of leakage and NOT pure
  overfitting — a real above-null signal separates ADD from Other. This does NOT
  establish disease biology: ADD is 100% Kerchner cohort (finding 0001), so the
  unit-level shuffle permutes the partition that is simultaneously "ADD" and
  "Kerchner", and an above-null result cannot separate disease from collection
  site. EXPLORATORY.

    ## Reading (for the finding prose; legend sits directly beneath the figure)
    In both panels the null AUC histogram is centred on chance (0.5) while the
  observed AUC (vermillion line) sits far to the right at ~0.96, with only a
  wide gap between the null mass and the marker — the empirical permutation p is
  0.001 for both models. This establishes that the ADD-vs-Other discrimination
  is above what label-shuffling produces, so it is not attributable to leakage
  or pure overfitting. It does not, however, license a disease-biology claim:
  because ADD and the Kerchner collection site are the same 40-sample partition
  (finding 0001), the permutation test cannot distinguish disease from site, so
  the above-null signal is confound-bounded and exploratory.

    Cohort/site confound probe. Observed ROC-AUC of a leakage-safe elastic-net
  logistic classifier vs its 1000-permutation label-shuffle null, from the

  plasma-EV proteome. Two panels on a shared AUC x-axis. TOP: predicting

  means the on-Cohort correction removed detectable within-non-ADD site
  structure; it does NOT clear the ADD classifier, because ADD is single-cohort
  (100% Kerchner, finding 0001) so ITS correction was EXTRAPOLATED, not
  estimated (finding 0004) — absence of residual site signal among
  cohort-spanning groups cannot prove the ADD separation is disease rather than
  site. Small/imbalanced (n=27, 7 vs 20) — a bounded probe with wide CIs.

    ### Reading (for the finding prose; legend sits directly beneath)
    The top panel asks whether the delivered proteome can still predict
  collection site where site is not aliased with disease: the observed site AUC
  (blue line, 0.475) falls squarely inside its label-shuffle null (permutation
  p=0.98), so among non-ADD samples the corrected proteome does not predict
  Kerchner-vs-Poston — within-non-ADD site structure was scrubbed by the
  on-Cohort correction. The bottom reference panel is the deliberate contrast:
  the ADD-vs-Other classifier's observed AUC (vermillion, 0.956) sits far beyond
  its null (p=0.001). Read together they establish that the confound probe
  collapses to null exactly where the ADD classifier beat it — but this is an
  honesty check, not exoneration: because ADD is single-cohort its correction
  was extrapolated rather than estimated, so a clean within-non-ADD site result
  cannot license a disease interpretation of the ADD separation.

❯ regarding: Pathway/compartment enrichment (GO-CC especially) on the           
  discriminative set.  Which discriminative set do you have in mind?            

● Good — it's exactly the right thing to pin down, because enrichment is only as
  meaningful as the query list, and we have a few candidate "discriminative
  sets," each with a different bias. Two things are fixed regardless: the
  background is our detected proteome (2340 proteins, not the whole genome), and
  I'd enrich up-in-ADD and down-in-ADD separately (our signal is directional —
  mostly down — and mixing directions blurs the biology). The remaining choice
  is which set:

● User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Which discriminative set should the GO/pathway enrichment run on? 
     (Background = the 2340 detected proteins; up-in-ADD and down-in-ADD 
     enriched separately either way.) → Can you tell direction from Boruta 
     analysis? If so, I think we should use Boruta's sets.

● Sharp question, and the answer is no — Boruta doesn't give direction natively.
  Boruta is an all-relevant relevance test: it compares each feature's
  Random-Forest importance against shadow (permuted) features and labels it
  Confirmed / Tentative / Rejected. That importance is unsigned — a magnitude,
  not a direction — so Boruta tells you a protein carries ADD-vs-Other
  information, not whether it's higher or lower in ADD.

  But we can attach direction cleanly, because we already have it: each
  protein's DE log2FC sign (ADD − Other) from finding 0006 is a well-defined
  per-feature direction, and it agreed with the elastic-net coefficient signs
  100% on the overlapping features — so it's a reliable direction label. So the
  plan becomes:

  1. Run Boruta on ADD-vs-Other → the Confirmed (all-relevant) set.
  2. Label each Confirmed protein up/down from its DE log2FC sign.
  3. Enrich Confirmed-up and Confirmed-down separately (background = the 2340
     detected proteins).

  One honest caveat: the direction is the marginal (univariate) sign — a protein
  that's relevant only through an interaction could have an ambiguous marginal
  direction. That's uncommon for plasma proteins, and I'll flag any Confirmed
  feature whose DE effect is near zero rather than force it into an up/down bin.
  (And the same site-confound ceiling still applies to why these proteins
  differ — Boruta characterizes what the discriminative set is, not
  disease-vs-site.)

  That fulfills what you want (Boruta's all-relevant set) with a defensible
  direction split. Let me start the Boruta run — it's minutes-slow, so I'll
  cache it; then I'll bring you the Confirmed set + direction breakdown before
  we run the enrichment.

    ## BORUTA all-relevant selection — ADD vs Other (plasma-EV) — DONE
  (statistician)

    **Question.** Which proteins are *all jointly relevant* to the ADD-vs-Other
  partition (the all-relevant complement to the elastic-net minimal-optimal
  set), and what is each Confirmed feature's differential-abundance direction?

    **Method (canonical choice).** Boruta all-relevant selection
  (`boruta_select`, seeded from `lib/analysis/boruta`@0.1). Real features
  compete against shadow (permuted-copy) features over a balanced Random Forest;
  each is Confirmed/Tentative/Rejected by the binomial test vs the best shadow.
  The shadow features **are the built-in null**, so — per
  `conventions/statistics.md` §B — this legitimately runs **once on the whole
  experimental matrix, no CV, no external null, no `run_null`**. Feeding it the
  median-normalized + ComBat/limma-corrected matrix is legitimate here
  (selection, not a per-feature significance test).

    ### Run configuration
    - Whole experimental matrix: 40 samples × 2340 proteins, log2, complete (no
  NaN). Target built on `Condition` as a **categorical** `add_vs_other` column
  (ADD="ADD" positive / {PDD,PDCN,HCN}="Other") so **classification** is
  inferred from the dtype — a numeric 0/1 column would have mis-inferred
  regression. Split verified 10 ADD / 30 Other.
    - **Default `max_iter=100, alpha=0.05, perc=100`, seed 20260915 — LANDED
  WELL, no tuning applied.** Converged in 99 iterations, shadow_threshold
  0.0053. 40 Confirmed is neither too few (no need to raise max_iter / lower
  perc) nor too large for downstream enrichment (no need to lower alpha).
  Runtime ~16 s (not "minutes-slow" on this matrix, but still cached per
  discipline). Boruta did **not** misbehave on this p≫n matrix.

    ### Results
    - **Counts: 40 Confirmed / 15 Tentative / 2285 Rejected.**
    - **Direction split of the 40 Confirmed: 18 up-in-ADD / 22 down-in-ADD / 0

  BAHD1, NFX1, VP13A). These are the co-regulated/correlated real signal the L1

    ### Leakage / null / CV safeguards
    - All-relevant selection with the **built-in shadow null**
  (relevance-vs-noise), so no CV and no external label-shuffle null are
  applicable — as the template/convention prescribe. `class_weight="balanced"`
  handles the 10-vs-30 imbalance.

    ### Caveats (stated, load-bearing)
    - **EXPLORATORY.** Small n — only 10 positives. The shadow null addresses
  relevance-vs-noise, not sampling stability at this n.
    - **ADD ≡ Kerchner cohort confound (hard; finding 0001).** Among these 40
  samples "ADD" and "Kerchner" are the SAME partition, so the Confirmed set is
  all-relevant to the **ADD-vs-Other partition, which is aliased with collection
  site** — NOT necessarily all-relevant to disease. The shadow null does
  **not** resolve this confound.
    - Matrix already batch-corrected on Cohort (finding 0004); direction labels
  come from the moderated DE contrast whose q are anticonservative (that caveat
  rides with the DE result, not with the Boruta decisions, which carry no q).

  The report follows:
    # GO/KEGG over-representation of the Boruta Confirmed set — ADD vs Other
  (directional)

    ## Question
    Do the Boruta all-relevant **Confirmed** proteins for the ADD-vs-Other
  contrast — split by DE direction — show over-representation of any GO
  (BP/MF/CC) term or KEGG pathway relative to the experiment's detected
  proteome? Run **up-in-ADD** and **down-in-ADD** separately. In particular,
  does the down-in-ADD set test out as intracellular/organellar (proteasome,
  mitochondrion, ER/Golgi), and is anything secreted/EV-related enriched?

    ## Method (and why it is the canonical choice)
    Over-representation analysis (ORA) via **g:Profiler** (`hsapiens`), seeded
  from the vetted template `lib/analysis/enrichment` (`enrich(query, background,
  organism=…)`). Directional ORA (up/down separately) is the convention — far
  more interpretable than a merged list. Sources: **GO:BP, GO:MF, GO:CC, KEGG**.
  Correction: **g:SCS** (g:Profiler's graph-structure-aware default; the
  scientist's choice). Background = the experiment's **detected, mappable
  proteome** (`domain_scope="custom"`, the 2340 analysis-subset accessions) —
  not the whole genome; the query is enforced to be a subset. Confirmed set
  round-tripped from the Boruta cache (`results/boruta/762e16f294e3/`,
  `BorutaResult.confirmed_features`); direction taken from the moderated DE
  contrast (`effect` = log2FC ADD−Other).

    ## Result — structured measurements
    **No term is significant in either direction. Every g:SCS-corrected p =
  1.0** (not merely above 0.05 — literally 1.0 for all tested terms, both
  directions).

    | Direction | n query (recognized) | GO:BP tested/sig | GO:CC tested/sig |
  GO:MF tested/sig | KEGG tested/sig | min p (g:SCS) |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | up-in-ADD | 18 / 18 | 783 / 0 | 172 / 0 | 178 / 0 | 76 / 0 | 1.0 |
    | down-in-ADD | 22 / 22 | 992 / 0 | 150 / 0 | 177 / 0 | 100 / 0 | 1.0 |

    Correction = g:SCS; family = all terms tested per source (as reported by
  g:Profiler); n = 18 (up) / 22 (down) query proteins vs 2340-protein custom
  background; g:Profiler effective domain = 2593 genes (the 2340 accessions
  expand to more genes because some protein-group first-members / accessions map
  to multiple genes). This is the ORA analogue of "no bare p": every tested
  term carries its corrected p + intersection/term sizes + gene ratio + the
  query genes hitting it, written to CSV.

    **Every test run is reported** — the full per-term tables (1209 up, 1419
  down) are written to disk, not just the (empty) significant sets.

    ## Top GO:CC compartment story (honest reading — nothing survives)
    The qualitative "intracellular/organellar, mostly down in ADD" impression is
  **not supported as an enrichment**. The down-in-ADD proteins *are*
  individually intracellular/organellar — nominal GO:CC hits include
  **proteasome complex** (3/50: PSMD3, TERA, PRS10), **ER** (5/478),
  **cytoplasmic/intracellular vesicle** (7/690), Golgi membrane, phagophore
  assembly site (ATG3, autophagy), plus mito complex-I subunit NDUV2 — but none
  is over-represented (all p=1.0). The up-in-ADD set likewise hits ER (7/478),
  Golgi, vesicle, chromatin, spindle — also all p=1.0. Crucially, **both** sets
  also hit "extracellular exosome / extracellular region / extracellular
  organelle": the background is itself a plasma-EV proteome dominated by
  intracellular-plus-EV proteins, so the discriminative proteins' compartment
  membership simply mirrors the detected proteome — there is no *selective*
  compartment enrichment in either direction. **Nothing
  secreted/extracellular/EV-related is enriched either** (same p=1.0). The
  compartment observation reflects proteome composition, not selection.

    ## Interpretation caveats (stated honestly)
    - **EXPLORATORY / underpowered.** 18 and 22 proteins are small queries;
  g:SCS over a detected-proteome background is conservative, and at this size a
  null result is largely a power statement — absence of enrichment is not
  evidence of absence.
    - **CONFOUND CEILING (hard, finding 0001).** The Confirmed set is
  all-relevant to the ADD-vs-Other partition, which is aliased with collection
  site (ADD is 100% Kerchner cohort). Even had terms survived, ORA would only
  characterize *what compartment/pathway the discriminative proteins belong to*
  — it does not establish disease biology (could be site/handling). Enrichment
  describes the set; it does not resolve disease-vs-site.

    ## Safeguards applied
    - Detected-proteome custom background (not whole genome); query enforced
  subset of background.
    - Directional ORA (up/down separate); g:SCS correction, named and recorded.
    - Decode signed-off parser; **decode 100% clean: 40/40 query, 2340/2340
  background** (2337 swissprot, 3 trembl; 0 unmappable). Protein groups resolved
  to the first member's accession (e.g. `sp|Q70IA8|MOB3C_HUMAN / …` → Q70IA8).
    - **g:Profiler data version recorded: `e114_eg62_p19_27110d83`** (pinned in
  provenance; the service is live/versioned).
    - Network is real; a failure **raises** `EnrichmentServiceError` — no silent
  empty result was faked. The run succeeded live.
    - Direction split asserted 18/22/0 as a data-change guard; background
  asserted = 2340.

    ## Suggested framing for the orchestrator
    The clean, defensible finding here is a **negative-with-a-reason**: the
  ADD-vs-Other discriminative proteins carry **no GO/KEGG over-representation
  over the detected proteome** under g:SCS, in either direction — and the
  "intracellular/organellar, down-in-ADD" impression is explained by proteome
  composition (the plasma-EV background is itself intracellular/EV-rich), not by
  selective enrichment. This both refutes the qualitative compartment claim as
  an *enrichment* and is consistent with the small-query power limit and the
  site confound. If a positive-leaning read is wanted, the more permissive
  `fdr`/`bonferroni` corrections could be run as a recorded sensitivity check,
  but given all g:SCS p=1.0 that is unlikely to change the conclusion — I did
  not run them without direction to.

● Agent "Record enrichment finding" finished · 3m 16s

● You're absolutely right, and it's an important correction — thank you for
  catching it. Let me state it precisely because it changes how finding 0009
  should read:

  Over-representation analysis (ORA, what g:Profiler ran) asks: of my discrete 
  set of 40 "hit" proteins, are more of them members of pathway X than you'd 
  expect by count? It's a membership/count test on a thresholded set — it
  ignores effect magnitude and abundance entirely, and with only 18/22 proteins
  it's underpowered. So an ORA null means "this small discrete set isn't
  count-enriched for any term." It does not mean "no pathway is coordinately
  shifted."

  What you're describing — a pathway that's over-represented by abundance/effect
  — is exactly what a rank-based GSEA would test: rank all 2340 proteins by
  their ADD-vs-Other effect and ask whether any pathway's members are
  coordinately shifted up or down across the whole list, weighted by magnitude.
  GSEA routinely finds coordinated pathway signal where ORA is null, precisely
  because it uses every protein and its effect size rather than a thresholded
  count. We did not run that.

  So my "bottom line #2 — not a specific pathway program" overstated what we
  established. The correct statement is narrower: the discriminative-protein set
  shows no count-based over-representation; a coordinated, abundance-weighted 
  pathway shift has not been tested and remains possible.

  I won't run GSEA (you asked me not to), but I will correct the record so
  finding 0009 doesn't overstate — that's honesty maintenance, not new analysis.
  Scoping it now:

● findings-workflow:findings-manager(Scope finding 0009 claim)
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

● Once that scoping lands, finding 0009 will read correctly: no count-based
  over-representation of the discriminative set, explicitly not a claim about
  abundance-weighted pathway signal — with a note that a rank-based GSEA is the
  right tool if you ever want to test that (available whenever you say, not run
  now).

  That leaves the earlier branch still open — you flagged the correction rather
  than picking a thread. So, whenever you're ready, the threads are:

  - Four-arm ADD-specificity check (my recommendation) — is the ADD signature
    ADD-specific or dementia-generic (also present in PDD)? It's what the
    PDD/PDCN arms were designed for, and it sharpens the primary finding.
  - Secondary PD contrast (PDD+PDCN vs others) — your stated secondary aim (more
    site-confounded, same ceiling).
  - Promote scripts + validate 0007 — move the scratch scripts to promoted/ so
    the headline classifier finding can go through blinded validation
    (/findings-workflow:stage5-validate 7).
  - (Held) abundance-weighted GSEA — the pathway test you just identified,
    whenever you want it.

  Which would you like — or point me somewhere else?
